## Changelog

- **2026-06-16 (Phase 6 — evidence-driven perf pass: profiled hot paths, narrowed the one unoptimized read; partitioning/cache deferred with measurements)** — Profiled the hot query paths on the live dev DB (15,149 budget_lines / 2,807 BS / 3,571 CF / 1,274 IVs) before touching anything. **Finding: the DB is not the bottleneck at current scale** — the analytics budgetLine query runs in ~2.7ms (EXPLAIN: Bitmap Index Scan on `budget_lines_planId_idx`), matrix IVs ~6.2ms via the `(organizationId,companyId,period)` composite, and existing indexes already cover every hot WHERE/orderBy. The matrix route and `recompute-data-source.ts` already use narrow `select`s. **The only unoptimized hot read was `/api/budgeting/analytics`** — its budgetLine load used `include: { costType: true, budgetDept: true, account }`, i.e. `SELECT *` + full related rows, hydrating ~30 wide columns × thousands of lines per dashboard load. **Fix:** replaced it with a single-source-of-truth `BUDGET_LINE_SELECT` (only the ~15 columns the handler reads + narrow per-line relation selects); the payload type is derived from that const so any access to a non-selected field is a **compile error** (tsc-driven completeness — verified the handler reads nothing more). Measured **81.6ms → 54.8ms (~1.5×, −27ms)** on the 2700-line displayed plan; scales linearly toward the 60-company target. Zero data-mutation, no migration, no stale-data risk. Profiling found the relation-narrowing alone was a no-op (≈0ms — costType/budgetDept are tiny tables; the cost is per-row column hydration), so only the column-narrowing shipped. **Deliberately deferred with rationale (NOT done):** 6.2 analytics Redis cache (premature at 8/60 cos AND a cache layer over financial aggregates re-introduces the stale-number trust risk just fixed — needs bulletproof invalidation before it's safe); 6.3 table partitioning (premature; no slow queries exist to justify it); index tuning (no missing indexes on any hot path). tsc 0; budgeting-API + risk suites **2353 green**.
- **2026-06-16 (Durable import fix — derive-delete-from-write: clean-slate scope computed from the inserted rows, not caller params; over-deletion now structurally impossible)** — Roadmap follow-up to the safety-net guard (below). Where the guard turned silent collateral deletion into a loud abort, this makes the *scope itself correct by construction*: each batch's clean-slate WHERE now derives its destructive scope from the DISTINCT identity in the INSERTED rows, so the delete touches exactly the footprint being overwritten and the guard becomes a redundant tripwire. **Changes** (verified file-by-file before editing — the multi-agent design plan was corrected where it was wrong): `import-batch.ts` (BudgetLine) archive+purge company scope → `footprintCompanyIds` (rows), planId already rows-derived, period/year stays caller-controlled; `bs-import-batch.ts` (BS) archive+purge planId → `footprintPlanIds` (rows); `kpi-import-batch.ts` (OperationalFact, hard-delete) delete company → `footprintCompanyIds`, metric already rows-derived, date window stays caller-controlled (intentional full-year reset); `production-adapter-handlers-soft.ts` (İcmal BudgetLine hard-delete) scoped by `companyId IN footprintCompanyIds` + guard (was `planId+sourceDocument` only — a partial İcmal re-import silently wiped sibling companies); `production-adapter-handlers-financial.ts` (pl_ebitda) + `organizationId` + guard. Reconciliation reads (`defaultReadActualSums` / BS / KPI) re-scoped to the rows' footprint so a surviving sibling isn't read as a false "extra". **Deliberately NOT changed:** CF (`cf-import-batch.ts` — its `entityScope` is already row-derived via `entityPrefixes`; exact-sourceId would risk under-deletion) and actuals (`actuals-import-batch.ts` — its only over-deletion vector is the period dimension = intentional full-year reset = a finance-semantic decision, deferred to the user). **Process:** ran two cloud workflows — an 8-file understand/design analysis, then a 4-lens adversarial review (data-loss / under-deletion-stale / guard-false-positive / behavior-recon) which returned SHIP (zero real bugs; all narrowing monotonic toward less deletion). The collateral guard then caught a real incomplete-refactor bug mid-implementation (a `replace_all` missed the BS archive's planId due to indentation — guard tripped archived>footprint, exactly its job). **Codex final gate caught a blocker the Claude review missed:** scoping the pl_ebitda delete to only the nonzero `monthly` dates left stale facts for months the source had zeroed out (the parser omits zero months) → year-sum overcount. Fixed by deleting the full parsed year (Codex re-confirmed SHIP). **Tests:** +4 derive-from-write sibling-isolation regressions (BudgetLine/BS/KPI: broader caller scope writes only the rows' scope, sibling survives, recon green); the 2 prior "guard ABORTS on broader companyIds" tests rewritten to the new safe behavior; mocks gained `count`. tsc 0; full `src/lib/onboarding/` suite **632 green**. **Deferred follow-ups (tracked):** actuals partial-year reset semantic (user decision); a fixture-level pl_ebitda zero-month-clearing regression test.
- **2026-06-16 (Safety net — import clean-slate collateral-deletion GUARD across all batch tables + KPI hard-delete scoped to its metrics)** — Follow-up to the cross-plan budget-wipe (below): the user flagged recurring import data-corruption as the project's #1 problem and asked for the systemic cause, not another point-fix. **Root cause:** every import "clean-slates" (delete matching rows → insert), but the DELETE scope is hand-written per batch and must EXACTLY match the INSERT identity; there was no shared abstraction deriving one from the other and no post-import invariant against collateral deletion — so each new identity dimension (companyId on BS rows, budget+actual plans per year, per-entity sourceId on CF) silently drifted the delete broader than the insert → sibling data loss (2026-06-11 BudgetLine cross-plan; 2026-05-31 BS/CF cross-entity) or double-count (2026-06-03 readers missing deletedAt). DB evidence of recurrence: 5913 archived BudgetLine + 959 BS + 1199 CF rows across 3 actors, 05-20…06-11. **Fix (user chose "safety net now"):** (1) NEW `src/lib/onboarding/collateral-guard.ts` — table-agnostic `assertNoCollateralDeletion` throws `CollateralDeletionError` (rolls back the write tx) when `archivedCount > footprintLiveCount`, where the footprint is counted INDEPENDENTLY from the INSERTED rows' own identity (companies/plans/entities/metrics), NOT from the archive WHERE (non-circular, so it actually fires on drift). Wired into all four batches: `import-batch.ts` (BudgetLine — catches caller companyIds/planIds broader than rows), `bs-import-batch.ts` (BalanceSheetLine — caller planIds broader than rows), `cf-import-batch.ts` (CashFlowEntry — dropped entity-prefix scope), `kpi-import-batch.ts` (OperationalFact — caller companyIds broader than rows). (2) **KPI hard-delete fix** (the one irreversible path — OperationalFact has no soft-delete): the reset now scopes `deleteMany` AND the reconciliation read to `metric ∈ rows' metrics`, so re-importing one KPI no longer wipes the company's sibling metrics, and a zero-row parse deletes nothing (`metric: { in: [] }`) instead of wiping the whole date window. Semantics note: a metric absent from the new file now lingers (file is authoritative only for the metrics it parses) — accepted vs irreversible loss; an explicit delete-intent manifest is the follow-up if full replacement is ever needed. (3) **Codex nit** — added `organizationId` to the per-year `pl_ebitda` deleteMany in `production-adapter-handlers-financial.ts` (defense-in-depth cross-org guard on a destructive delete). **Tests:** +5 guard unit + 1 BudgetLine guard-trip + 3 KPI (metric-scope survives sibling / zero-row no-op / guard-trip) regression cases; mocks gained `count` + honor planId/metric/year; verified guards FAIL without the fix. tsc 0; full `src/lib/onboarding/` suite **631 green** + ai-import **175 green**. Reviewed by Codex (SHIP-WITH-NITS). **Known residual (tracked, not blocking):** CF guard can't catch a bad/shared sourceTag or the no-entityCode legacy fallback (no row-level key to compare); `production-adapter-handlers-soft.ts` İcmal delete (planId+sourceDocument) is outside the batch guard. Next durable step (deferred): derive-delete-from-write shared helper so the guard rarely needs to fire.
- **2026-06-16 (Fix — Excel import clean-slate wiped the SIBLING same-year plan; recovered 2700 budget lines + scoped the archive to its target plan)** — User opened `/budgeting`, saw "100% icra / 100% composite score" and asked if it was real. It was not: the page had auto-selected the **"Azərşəkər 2026 Actuals"** plan (because the **"2026 Budget" plan was empty — 0 live lines**), and the analytics route reports `actual = the plan's own lines` for a `kind="actual"` plan (`analytics/route.ts:336-347`), so execution reads a tautological 100% (and the gauge's "target 46%" is just `elapsedPct`). **Root cause (verified by timestamp + `deletedBy` + code):** the AI Multi-File Import on **2026-06-11 10:53** soft-deleted all **2700** lines of the 2026 Budget plan (`deletedBy="ai-multi-import"`, same instant the 2026 Actuals plan was written). `runImportBatch`'s clean-slate archive (`import-batch.ts`) scoped the soft-delete by `organizationId + companyId + plan.year` **but NOT by `planId`** — so importing actuals into the 2026 ACTUALS plan also archived the 2026 BUDGET plan's lines (same company, same year). **Recovery:** the 2700 lines were soft-deleted (not purged), so restored them by clearing `deletedAt/deletedBy` — 2026 Budget back to 2700 live lines (rev 72.0M / cogs 38.7M / opex 19.0M); execution now reads a real **13.2% revenue / 23.9% expense** (Jan–Apr actuals vs full-year budget) instead of 100%. **Fix:** added `planId: { in: planIds }` to the archive AND purge WHERE clauses — a re-import now clean-slates only the plan it writes to; sibling same-year plans (budget vs actual) are untouched. Empty `planIds` (no rows) → `{ in: [] }` archives nothing, which also closes the delete-without-reinsert footgun. The change can only NARROW the destructive query, never widen it. **Completeness check (sibling batch fns):** BS (`bs-import-batch.ts:209`) already scopes by `planId`; CF (`cash_flow_entries`) has no `planId` column (identity is `source`+`sourceId`+year); KPI (`operational_facts`) has no plan/soft-delete — only `BudgetLine` was vulnerable. **Tests:** +2 regression cases (cross-plan import leaves the sibling plan's lines live + recon green; same-plan re-import still clean-slates its own prior lines) — the mock `updateMany`/`deleteMany` now honor `planId`/`plan.year` so the guard is real (verified it FAILS without the fix: budget lines → 0 live). 17 import-batch + 62 related batch tests green; tsc 0. **Note:** the 2026 Actuals plan carries data only for Jan–Apr (May–Jun not yet loaded — actuals are ~2 months stale).
- **2026-06-05 (Feature — manual FINANCIAL-variable entry (inventory) in Indicator Health, generalizable registry; +2 architect P1 integrity fixes)** — User ("подтверждаю"): some indicators are gappy because a *financial* figure (period-end inventory) was never imported, and re-uploading Excel to fix one number is heavy — let the user type it inline, like the operational-KPI inline form (#33). Built the financial counterpart end-to-end. **Root insight (verified against the live resolver, no engine change):** `FP_INVENTORY_TURNS` (formula `cogs / inventory`) reads `inventory` via the active `balanceSheetLine.inventory` resolver (`recompute-resolvers-b.ts`, Phase 7.O), which SUMS current-asset rows whose `account.name` matches an inventory heuristic, on the year's `kind="actual"` plan at `month=12`. So unblocking it needs only a *conforming BalanceSheetLine write* — no recompute-engine touch (the «финансовое ядро» stays untouched). **Built:** (1) `src/lib/risk/financial-variable-rules.ts` — generalizable registry (`variable`/`formulaVariable`/`requiredInputKey`/`lineType`/`subType`/canonical CoA/bounds/`unlocks`) + pure `validateFinancialValue`; inventory ships first as the proven template, receivables/payables/cash add as DATA later. (2) `POST /api/budgeting/financial-variable` — manager+, Zod, cross-tenant guard, 2-step `forceConfirm`, find-or-create the year's actual plan + canonical "Inventory" CoA, upsert the BS line, `recomputeAfterDataChange`, audit — faithfully mirroring `/api/operational-facts`. (3) `FinancialVariableEntry.tsx` inline form (company + value + **year picker** + live recompute count + "unlocks" line) wired into `IndicatorHealthView` beside the operational form via `resolveFinancialVariable` (cta.ts). (4) i18n EN/RU/AZ (`adminIndicatorHealth.financialEntry`, 19 keys × 3, parity-checked). **Live-verified (no synthetic data written):** route reachable + auth-gated (401, not 404 — turbopack picked it up); DB read confirms **48 real `FP_INVENTORY_TURNS` inventory gaps** (CPC/AZSF/PROMALT/MALT, 2020-2026) the form attaches to. **Wiring bug caught by live-checking the emitted string:** the health scan's `extractMissingVariable` regexes the BARE formula variable (`"inventory"`) out of "undefined variable: inventory", NOT the qualified `"balanceSheetLine.inventory"` — so the matcher now keys on the bare name (and accepts the qualified key for robustness); without this the form would never have rendered. **Architect security review (new-API-route risk class) found 2 P1s + 1 P2, all fixed before commit:** (P1a) no period-lock check → a manager could overwrite inventory on a CFO-locked closed year; added the same `findFirstActiveLockInPeriods(containingPeriodKeys(year,12))` → **423** guard the cash-flow route uses. (P1b) the resolver SUMS all inventory-named current assets but the route deduped only on its own CoA → if inventory already exists under a different account for the picked year, a manual write would silently double-count and HALVE the indicator (the year-picker makes this reachable); added a pre-write detection across all actual plans (reusing the now-exported `bsIsInventoryLine` — single source of truth) that folds a clear "already recorded … double-count" warning into the confirm-gate. (P2) the auto-created fallback plan no longer claims a fake `status:"approved"` (now `"draft"`, reads ignore status) and its creation is audited. **Gates:** tsc 0; **49** new/extended unit+handler tests (registry/validation, resolver-contract lock, cta resolver, form helpers, route auth/validation/lock-423/double-count/conformance); full suite **5748 green**; i18n parity ✓. **User step (not synthetic-able by me):** log in → Indicator Health → expand an inventory gap → enter a *real* period-end inventory figure → watch `FP_INVENTORY_TURNS` recompute (I won't fabricate a number into the shared financial DB, and can't enter credentials).
- **2026-06-03 (Terminal bug-audit (multi-agent) — P1 fix: AI narratives + AI drill tool double-counted financials after re-import)** — User asked for a thorough terminal test → ran an adversarially-verified multi-agent audit (7 dimensions; 5 re-running after a structured-output glitch). Top finding (P1): the AI-Analytics collectors (`src/lib/ai/section-context.ts` computePL / computeBalanceSheet / computeCashFlow) and the AI agent's `get_account_drill` tool (`src/lib/ai/tools.ts:246`) read BudgetLine / BalanceSheetLine / CashFlowEntry **without `deletedAt: null`**. Those tables soft-delete-then-reinsert on re-import under the same planId, so after any re-import the AI client-facing narratives + drill totals summed archived + live rows → inflated revenue/COGS/EBITDA/assets/cash (the sibling UI routes that DO filter cite measured **×6.27 budgetLine / ×1.98 CF** on live AzerSheker data). Fixed all 4 reads; COGS/assumptions/forecast collectors correctly left alone (no `deletedAt` column). tsc 0; 15 section-context + ai-analytics tests green. (7 more confirmed findings from the audit tracked in CARRYOVER — fix-first batches: rollup-cell filter sweep, period desync, drilldown kind:'actual', scenario stale-baseline, FX snapshot.)
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — scenario re-derive miscounted unknown-baseline indicators as "improved")** — User hit a What-if "error": an AZN devaluation (−20% manat, import costs ↑) read **"4 improved / 0 worsened"** — economically backwards, and all 4 rows had base `unknown` (0.00→0.00). Root cause in `scenario-rederive.ts`: `STATUS_ORDER` ranked `unknown=0` (worst), so an indicator with NO baseline that the shock's assumed-import-share gives a value flips `unknown→status` and counts as "improved" (any real status outranks unknown). Fix: a delta is `changed` (hence improved/worsened-classifiable) only when BOTH baseline and scenario statuses are REAL (non-null, non-`unknown`) — `unknown↔real` transitions are non-comparable and excluded (matches the UI preview's `statusRank = -1` convention). +1 regression test (unknown→green AND unknown→red both excluded from counts). **Verified LIVE** (restarted the dev LaunchAgent to load the lib change): BRENT_TO_140 re-derive 10 changed/5 improved/5 worsened (4 bogus `green→unknown`) → **6 changed / 4 improved / 2 worsened, 0 unknown rows counted**; survivors are real (FP_GROSS_MARGIN amber→red worsened). tsc 0; rederive tests green. **NOTE (separate, deeper):** whether each shock moves each indicator in the economically-correct DIRECTION (e.g. a Brent oil spike showing some grain-cost signals improve) is a transmission-correctness question for a dedicated review — not this classification bug.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — LEGACY scenario simulator had the SAME unknown→"improved" bug, on the path the seeded scenarios actually use)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`, 8 more adversarially-verified findings) surfaced that the unknown-classification fix above only patched the **driver path** (`scenario-rederive.ts`). The **legacy multiplier path** (`src/lib/risk/scenario-simulator.ts` `simulateScenario`) had the identical `STATUS_ORDER.unknown=0` defect — and it's the path **every seeded `adjustments` scenario** (SUGAR_PRICE_DROP_20, DROUGHT_2026, AZN_DEVAL_15, …) reaches via the always-rendered "Süratli hesablama"/quickCalc button (no `mode=drivers`; the drivers button is hidden for adjustments-only scenarios). A crisis (multiply/delta) applied to a no-data indicator (persisted value:0, status:`unknown`) reclassified to a real band (`classifyValue(0)` is finite) and scored as an **improvement**, inflating `improved`. Fix: `changed` now requires BOTH ends real (`baselineStatus !== "unknown" && scenarioStatus !== "unknown"`); the same flag gates `buildDeltaMap`, so unknown rows no longer paint a fake status flip on the HeatMap overlay. +4 regression tests (`scenario-simulator.test.ts`). tsc 0; 61 scenario tests green. (13 more confirmed audit findings tracked in CARRYOVER — next P1s: SSE postgres-listener connect-rejection poisoning that kills `/api/events/stream` process-wide, and scoped-manager audit-log pagination truncation.)
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — SSE live-update listener permanently poisoned by one first-connect failure; explains the live `/api/events/stream` 503)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) P1 #2. `src/lib/events/postgres-listener.ts` `getClient()` assigned the connect IIFE to the module `clientPromise` singleton **before** `await client.connect()` resolved. The only reset (`clientPromise = null`) lived inside the `client.on('error')` handler — which is registered *after* a successful connect and fires on a client *event*, never on a `connect()`/`LISTEN()` **promise** rejection. So a single DB cold-start/restart/blip at the moment of the first subscribe after a deploy cached a permanently-**rejected** promise; every later `subscribe()` re-threw, the SSE route emitted an `error` event and closed, and live updates stayed dead **process-wide until redeploy** (the feature silently degraded to REST polling — hence P1 not P0). Fix: wrapped connect + both LISTEN queries in a `try/catch` that nulls the singleton and tears down the half-open client on any failure, so the next `subscribe()` retries a fresh connection; this also heals the reconnect-connect path (a reconnect whose connect rejected previously re-poisoned the singleton the same way). The connected client is bound to a `const` so the long-lived handler closures keep a non-nullable reference. +2 regression tests (connect-rejection and LISTEN-query-rejection each reset the singleton + tear down the half-open client; 7 listener tests total). tsc 0.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — scoped (non-admin) manager's audit trail silently truncated; compliance gap)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) P1 #3. `GET /api/audit/events` fetched `take=limit+1` rows then applied the Phase 7.F sub-group RBAC filter **in JS** (the `IndicatorValue→company` map can't live in the Prisma `where` — `entityId` is a free-text polymorphic column, not a relation FK), but computed `hasMore`/`nextCursor` from the **post-filter** array. So whenever the limit+1 window contained an out-of-scope `Company`/`IndicatorValue` event (imports, threshold overrides, recomputes — among the most common audit rows), `scopedRows.length ≤ limit` → `hasMore=false`, `nextCursor=null`, and a scoped manager saw "no more pages" while in-scope events still existed deeper in the table, with no continuation token — a truncated audit trail. Fixed by **loop-paging**: fetch keyset batches (advancing the composite `(createdAt,id)` cursor by the last RAW row each round) until `limit+1` IN-SCOPE rows are collected or the window is exhausted, then derive `hasMore`/`nextCursor` from the in-scope set. `MAX_SCAN_ROUNDS=25` bounds the worst case for a sparse-scope manager; on the cap the handler returns a partial page plus a cursor at the last raw row so the client resumes the scan instead of dropping deeper in-scope rows. Admin (`scope.ids===null`) keeps the single-round fast path, and the existing orderBy/cursor/`take=limit+1` shape-lock tests still pass unchanged. +2 regression tests covering the previously-UNTESTED RBAC-filtered branch (no truncation across batches + correct exhaustion). tsc 0; 32 audit tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — aggregate rollup cells double-counted in 3 terminal UI surfaces)** — Terminal bug-audit run-1 #2/#3 (P2). The matrix API merges `parentCells` (`kind:'real-rollup'`) + `subgroupCells` (`kind:'synthetic-rollup'`) into the flat `cells` array and adds `subgroupCompanies` to `companies` (`matrix/route.ts:706-708`), so each sub-group / holding parent shows up as a row whose status is the worst-of-its-children. Four loops iterated `cells` with no `isAggregateRollup()` filter and so counted those parents alongside the very children they summarize: the HeatMap-model **alert-count** loop (inflated the `[alerts 🔔 N]` badge and wrongly flagged parent rows as "alerted" in CompanyTree's watchlist tab), the HeatMap-model **`summarizeMatrix`** call (double-counted the headline "12G / 5A / 2R" distribution), **`computeTopMovers`** in `movers.ts` (a parent's rollup sparkline surfaced the same movement a second time and, being larger-magnitude, often outranked the real leaf — affects both the HeatMap overlay and the TodayBrief top-5), and the **TodayBrief worst-red** loop (duplicated a child's breach in the "worst" list). Fixed all four via the canonical `isAggregateRollup()` gate — the same helper `alert-rules.ts`, `composite-score.ts`, and `ActionCenterPanel.tsx` already use, so the discriminated-union `kind` field is now honoured consistently across every consumer. `MatrixCellLike` in `movers.ts` gained the optional `kind` field. +1 movers regression test (rollup cells excluded despite larger deltas; 9 movers tests). Not visual-gated — no HeatMap/CompanyTree/PanelGrid/`*.module.css`/`globals.css`/`tailwind.config.ts` file was touched, and the change is data-correctness (which cells get counted), not layout. tsc 0; 38 risk + terminal tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — HeatMap freshness badge «Updated Xh ago» never rendered)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) P2 #7. `matrix/route.ts` had `computedAt: true` in the `select` for both the leaf `values` and the `parentValues` queries, but none of the three cell-building `.map()`s copied `computedAt` onto the emitted cell objects. The freshness aggregation loop read `(c as { computedAt?: ... }).computedAt` — the `as` cast silenced the TS error, so at runtime every `cellTs` was `undefined`, the loop always `continue`d, and `lastComputedAt` was returned as `null`. `HeatMap.tsx` gates the «Updated Xh ago» dot+label strictly on `data?.lastComputedAt`, so the data-staleness affordance on the production risk dashboard was permanently absent — operators got no "how stale is this" signal and could trust silently-stale auto-recomputed numbers. Fixed by computing `max(computedAt)` directly from the raw `values` + `parentValues` rows (which already select it), a zero-extra-query change that also avoids shipping `computedAt` on every cell. +1 handler regression test (lastComputedAt populated from an IV's computedAt; 16 matrix handler tests). tsc 0. **Visual note:** the badge now renders for the first time, so the terminal visual baseline (captured while it was absent due to this bug) will need regenerating with a `BASELINE UPDATE: freshness badge now renders` token on the next terminal-layout commit; `matrix/route.ts` is not itself in the visual-gate file list, and the badge content is relative-time text, so this is a layout-content addition, not a layout regression.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — indicator drilldown could diverge from the value it explains: missing `kind:"actual"` plan predicate)** — Terminal bug-audit run-1 #6 (P2). `GET /api/indicators/values/[id]/drilldown` filtered the explaining budget lines by `plan: { year: period.year }` only, but the recompute resolver that produced the indicator value uses `plan: { year, kind: "actual" }` (`recompute-data-source.ts:339`) — the terminal's realized P&L reads ACTUAL plans exclusively so a forward budget plan never double-counts into realized indicators. Until recently every plan defaulted to `kind:"actual"`, making this a no-op; but the Y3 actuals/budget split now creates genuine `kind:"budget"` forward plans, so the drilldown could sum actual+budget lines and overstate the "where does this number come from?" breakdown relative to the indicator it explains. Fixed by mirroring the resolver's predicate exactly (`plan: { year, kind: "actual" }`). +1 regression assertion locking the drilldown's `where.plan` shape. tsc 0; 7 drilldown handler tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — operationalFact "snapshot" latest-by-date tie-break was non-deterministic)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) P3 #8. `listOperationalFacts` (`recompute-data-source.ts`) ran `findMany` with NO `orderBy`, and the snapshot branch of `operationalFactResolver` (`recompute-resolvers-a.ts`) reduced with strict `>` (`r.date > latest.date`). On a same-date tie it kept whichever row appeared first in Postgres's unspecified row order. `OperationalFact` has no `@@unique` on `(companyId, metric, date)` and several writers use plain `create` (no delete-before-insert), so a value corrected at the same as-of date leaves two same-date rows — and which one "won" could flip between recomputes with no data change (e.g. `LEGAL_CASES_ACTIVE` oscillating). Fixed by adding `orderBy: [{ date: 'asc' }, { createdAt: 'asc' }]` to the query and changing the reducer to `>=` so that on a same-date tie the later (most-recently-written) row wins — deterministic, and semantically the correction rather than the stale original. Low blast radius (the dominant snapshot writers do delete-before-insert), hence P3. +1 regression test (same-date facts → latest-written wins; 6 resolver tests). tsc 0.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — What-if "worst-hit companies" chips ranked parent/holding rollups alongside their own children)** — Terminal bug-audit #6 (P2, both runs). `simulateByDrivers` builds `byCompany` over every company, and parent/holding scores come from `deriveParentComposites` — a revenue-weighted rollup of the SAME children. Neither worst-hit consumer filtered to leaves: the `ScenarioPanel` chips (`:631`) and the `/simulate` route's brief `worstHit` (`:173`). So a holding plus its biggest subsidiary surfaced as two chips for one underlying shock, crowding genuinely-distinct operating companies out of the top-N. The `isLeaf` predicate already existed inside the engine (`scenario-rederive.ts:178`, used to gate cell emission) but wasn't exposed on the `byCompany` rows. Fix: added `isLeaf: boolean` to `DriverCompositeSwing` (populated from the existing helper), `isLeaf?: boolean` to the terminal store's byCompany type (optional — untyped JSON ingest), and a leaf filter at both worst-hit sites using `isLeaf !== false` (treats a missing flag in a legacy payload as a leaf, preserving prior behaviour rather than hiding everything). Strengthened the route drivers test: the mocked `byCompany` now includes a parent row with a *bigger* score drop that must be excluded, so the existing length-1 / content assertions actively prove the leaf filter. Not visual-gated — `ScenarioPanel.tsx` is not in the HeatMap/CompanyTree/PanelGrid gate list and the change is which rows rank, not layout. tsc 0; 93 scenario tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — terminal period selector desynced the panels; only the HeatMap followed it — VERIFIED LIVE)** — Terminal bug-audit #4 (P2). `selectedPeriod` lived as HeatMap-LOCAL `useState` (a deliberate choice per the code comment "so panel switches don't ripple across other consumers of useMatrix()"). The consequence: picking a quarter/month re-scoped only Panel 2 (HeatMap), while every other panel — Today's Brief, Action Center, CompanyTree, CompanySnapshot, Compare, CommandBar — calls `useMatrix()` with no period and so stayed pinned to the annual `"__default__"` cache entry. Confirmed live via the matrix API: annual `"2026"` = 220 cells / Σ≈−162B vs `"2026-Q1"` = 27 cells / Σ≈8.5M — a user viewing Q1 saw the heatmap on Q1 but the brief/action-center/tree on full-year numbers. Because this reversed a documented design decision, asked the user (UX is their call); **they chose "all panels follow the period."** Lifted `selectedPeriod` + `setSelectedPeriod` into `terminalStore`; `useMatrix(period?)` now defaults `period` to the store's `selectedPeriod` (so no-arg consumers follow it); the HeatMap model reads/writes the store, its prop-seed guarded to `period !== undefined` so the production no-prop `<HeatMap/>` render and the hand-rolled store mocks in the HeatMap test suite don't fire the seed. **Verified LIVE via the Chrome connector:** clicking Q1 moved the HeatMap header to `2026-Q1` (counts 54/21/29→10/6/3) AND the alert strip (50→4) AND Panel-1 CompanyTree risk badges (AZSEKER R49→R33, EDEN R50→R95, AZSF R39→R9), while the period-independent readiness % stayed put — proving the side panels now re-scope together. +1 `use-matrix` regression test (no-arg `useMatrix()` follows the store-selected period, leak-safe reset). Not visual-gated — only the store + two hooks changed, and the default-annual render (the baseline state) is unchanged; the desync only ever manifested after a manual period pick. tsc 0; 669 terminal + 10 use-matrix tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — What-if scenarios showed spurious "improvements" on shock-unrelated indicators; the user's "BRENT oil-spike → wheat/grain cost improved")** — Terminal bug-audit #7 (P2). `simulateByDrivers` recomputed EVERY (company, indicator) pair with the shock overrides applied, then diffed the FRESH recompute against the STORED baseline `IndicatorValue`. For an indicator the shock does not touch — `FP_WHEAT_PRICE_SIGNAL` (formula `wheat_price_latest`) and `FP_GRAIN_COST_PRESSURE_BLEND` (`(corn_price_latest + wheat_price_latest)/2`), pure commodity-feed scalars; `resolveShockOverrides` emits only P&L scalars (revenue/cogs/total_input_cost/ebitda/…) — the fresh recompute re-read the CURRENT feed (237.5) while the stored baseline was STALE (380), manufacturing a red→green "improvement" with no causal link to the oil shock. Confirmed live via the Chrome connector against `BRENT_TO_140`: exactly those 4 rows (HORIZON + EDEN), `improvedWhereValueUNCHANGED: []` (the values genuinely moved, ruling out a classification bug), and `resolveShockOverrides` provably emits no wheat/corn key. Fix: a new `formulaTouchesOverride(formula, overrides)` helper word-boundary-matches the formula against the shock's ACTUAL override keys (so it correctly includes `yield_per_ha` for a yieldShock and excludes `opex`, which the static `FINANCIAL_SCALAR_RE` gets wrong for this purpose); the per-pair re-derive loop now SKIPS the recompute when the formula touches none of the shocked scalars, keeping the baseline so `changed` stays false. The genuinely-shocked margin/cost indicators still recompute. +1 regression test (a stale-baseline pure-feed indicator is skipped — recompute never called — and stays unchanged, while the margin indicator still worsens). Scope note: shock-AFFECTED indicators still diff a fresh scenario against the stored baseline, so a minor magnitude drift is still possible for them — a smaller follow-up; the user-visible defect (wholly-unrelated indicators flipping) is what this closes. **Verification honesty:** the local dev server's HMR did not reload this deep lib change, so the in-session live re-run still returned the pre-fix numbers; the logic is proven by the unit test and ships correct on the next Vercel build (a `kickstart` was avoided because it logs the user out of the live session). tsc 0; 8 scenario-rederive tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — EBITDA margin mixed raw-currency captured EBITDA with FX-converted revenue; latent)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) #4 (P2, latent). `budgetLineResolver` (`recompute-resolvers-b.ts`) preferred the source's own captured `pl_ebitda` operational-fact as the `IND_EBITDA_MARGIN` numerator, but `revenue` (the denominator) is FX-normalized to base currency by `aggregatePnlLines`, while `capture-plf-ebitda` stores `pl_ebitda` verbatim from the workbook cell with a hardcoded `unit`. For a non-base-currency entity this divides a raw-source-currency EBITDA by FX-converted revenue → a wrong margin. Fix: added `unit` to the `listOperationalFacts` select (+ optional `FactRow.unit`) and now only trust the captured subtotal when every `pl_ebitda` row's `unit` equals the company's base currency; on a currency mismatch it falls back to the FX-normalized `net_income + da_total` derivation (already in base ccy). Because the capture pipeline hardcodes the unit to AZN, a non-AZN base currency cleanly triggers the fallback; an absent unit is treated as "trust" so existing all-AZN behaviour is unchanged. +2 regression tests (USD-tagged capture ignored → derived 60% green; AZN-tagged capture trusted → 10% amber). No wrong number today — all four entities carrying `pl_ebitda` (CPC/AZSF/EDEN/MALT) are AZN — but the landmine is now disarmed ahead of the multi-currency roadmap. tsc 0; 161 recompute tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — booking resolver counted foreign bookings with no FX rate at face value; latent)** — Terminal bug-audit re-run (`we2d7o4h2`) #5 (P2, latent). The hospitality `bookingResolver` summed `revenueInBase(b)` — which silently falls back to `rate = 1` for a non-null currency lacking an `exchangeRate` — into `room_revenue`, `fx_revenue`, and `rev_by_country`, recording `missing_rate_count` but never skipping. The budgetLine path (`aggregatePnlLines`) does the opposite: it `continue`s on a foreign-no-rate line so a raw foreign amount never enters base-currency totals. Mirrored that: a foreign booking with no rate is now EXCLUDED from the revenue aggregates (occupancy — rooms/nights — still counts, being physical and currency-independent), so it can no longer inflate `fx_revenue_share` (→ `HOSP_FX_EXPOSURE`) or `source_country_hhi` (→ `HOSP_SOURCE_HHI`) with a wrong-scale number. Also fixed the fx classification: "foreign" is now `currencyCode != null && currencyCode !== baseCurrency` (was just `!= null`), so a booking tagged with the base currency is correctly domestic rather than counted into fx revenue. +2 regression tests (foreign-no-rate excluded from `room_revenue` yet still counted as an active booking; a base-currency-tagged booking yields `fx_revenue_share` 0.5 not 1.0). Blast radius nil today — hospitality is a demo/stub sector with zero production bookings — but the path is now consistent with the P&L aggregator. tsc 0; 163 recompute tests green.
- **2026-06-03 (Fix — company-financials-snapshot summed plannedAmount with no FX conversion + no org scope; latent — CLOSES the terminal bug-audit)** — Terminal bug-audit run-1 #8 (P3, latent). `getCompanyFinancialsSnapshot` (which feeds the intel crossing-scan's impact-forecast LLM with revenue/COGS/OPEX/EBITDA "in AZN") summed `budgetLine.groupBy._sum.plannedAmount` per `lineType` — a raw aggregate that would fold a foreign-currency line's plannedAmount straight into the AZN totals the return type promises. Switched `groupBy` → `findMany` + per-line FX normalization: a foreign line (`currencyCode != null && !== "AZN"`) with a rate is converted (`× exchangeRate`), with NO rate is SKIPPED (mirrors `aggregatePnlLines`, never summed at face value), and base/blank-currency lines pass through unconverted. Also added an optional `organizationId` parameter — now supplied by the crossing-scan-runner caller — pinned in the `where` for defense-in-depth (companyId is a globally-unique cuid, so its absence was never a live cross-tenant leak, but this matches every other budgetLine read). New test file with 6 cases (AZN sum + ebitda, USD→AZN conversion, skip-no-rate regression, org-scope pinned, org-omitted back-compat, no-data nulls). Dormant today (all entities AZN). tsc 0; 6 tests green. **This was the last of the 14 distinct findings from the two-run multi-agent terminal audit — all now fixed and pushed.**
- **2026-06-02 (Auto-update Stage 2 — Vercel Cron free-feed refresh + recompute + heartbeat)** — Completes option B. New `GET /api/cron/refresh-feeds` (`maxDuration=300`, `force-dynamic`): auth via `CRON_SECRET` bearer (503 if unset, 401 on mismatch — handler-tested), then per active org → pulls the FREE feeds (`getCommodityAdapters()`: CBAR/Yahoo/open-meteo/FAO/CPI/USDA; **NO paid LLM crawl**) via `ingestCommodityData`, recomputes operational companies for the current year via `runRecomputeForCompanies` (best-effort), and writes a heartbeat to `Org.settings` (`intelLastRunAt` / `intelLastRunPointsWritten` / `intelLastRunErrorCount` / `intelLastRunErrors`) as the durable, type-safe fail-alert (a stale `intelLastRunAt` or non-zero error count flags a silently-failing feed). `vercel.json` registers a daily 06:00 UTC cron. **User-side setup to activate:** set `CRON_SECRET` env on Vercel (+ optional `EIA_API_KEY`/`USDA_API_KEY` free keys); the 300s `maxDuration` needs Vercel **Pro** (Hobby ~10s is too short → upgrade or keep the manual "Запустить impact-scan" button). Could NOT runtime-test the live fetch (hits external APIs; cron fires only in deployed Vercel; sandbox blocks the feed hosts) — verified by tsc + the auth-gate handler test (3/3) + reuse of the existing tested ingest/recompute. tsc 0.
- **2026-06-02 (Auto-update Stage 1 — recompute-chaining: data change → indicators refresh)** — User chose "recompute + auto-refresh on prod" (via `decision-stress-test`; the 3-role chair converged on recompute-chaining as the clearly-worth-it core, AI-crawl stays manual, local daemon skipped). **Stage 1:** new `recomputeAfterDataChange(org, company, year)` (`src/lib/recompute/recompute-on-change.ts`) — **synchronous** (serverless freezes fire-and-forget after the response) + **best-effort** (a recompute failure never rolls back the committed write; stale IVs self-heal next run). Wired into the manual operational-fact `POST` (after upsert) + `DELETE /[id]` (after delete) so entering/removing a KPI immediately recomputes the company's indicators — closes the "data changed but the indicator didn't" gap (the feed path reuses the same helper in Stage 2). Reuses `runRecomputeForCompanies`. tsc 0; 14 operational-facts handler tests green (recompute is swallowed under test mocks — the best-effort contract holds). Stage 2 (Vercel Cron free-feed refresh + ingest recompute + fail-alert) next.
- **2026-06-02 (Data integrity — Data Sources cards show REAL feed values + date, not hardcoded examples)** — User flagged that the "СВЕЖИЙ ПРИМЕР" numbers (Brent $105.88, AZN 1.70) looked like live quotes but it was unclear if they came from the source or were made up. Confirmed: they were hardcoded `sampleLatest.value` in `sources-catalog.ts` (comment literally "Realistic example value"), decoupled from the feed — real Brent was **110.53 @ 2026-05-15**, not 105.88. Fix (user chose "show real + date"): new `GET /api/admin/source-latest` returns the genuine latest IntelDataPoint value + observation date per source's display metric; `DataSourcesCatalogView` renders it ("110.53 USD/bbl · на 15.05.2026") with an honest "нет свежих данных" fallback; section relabeled "Свежий пример" → "Последнее из источника" (so provenance + freshness are explicit). - **2026-06-02 (Fix — CF parser dropped the sign of refund/reversal months)** — `parsePlfCfSheet` took `Math.abs()` of every monthly value and forced direction by code segment (CF.01.01.*=inflow, CF.01.02.*=outflow). A POSITIVE month inside an outflow line (a refund/reversal) was therefore flipped into an outflow, overstating outflow by 2× the refund. Found via the 2026 actuals reconciliation: MALT operating CF off by 204,478 (= 2× a 102,239 Jan refund on CF.01.02.23), AZSF by 40,170, EDEN by 520. **Fix:** parser now keeps the SIGNED monthly value; the handler (`makeCfHandler`) derives the per-MONTH inflow/outflow direction from that sign (amount stays a positive magnitude). +1 regression test (refund month kept positive, nets correctly). Surgical data correction (`scripts/fix-cf-signs.cjs`): re-derived entryType from each month's file sign and flipped the 4 mis-signed CashFlowEntry rows — **operating CF now matches the file bit-perfect for all 4 entities** (CPC 3,410,136 / AZSF 411,104 / EDEN −1,771,046 / MALT 614,295). Single-sign lines unaffected. tsc 0; 1107 onboarding+budgeting tests green. **Full 2026 actuals reconciliation now clean: P&L, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow (all 3 sections), Land, CAPEX-total all tie to Guvven Fin.xlsx.**
- **2026-06-02 (Budget — load İcmal 2026 plan into the "2026 Budget" plan; execution % now computes)** — The user's 2026 budget was the **İcmal** sheet in `Farming strategy - Guvven.xlsx` (a full forward P&L plan 2026–2035). The AI import had ingested it as a *forward-forecast* (→ Organization.settings), not as monthly budget BudgetLines, so the budgeting module's "исполнение %" had nothing to compare. New `scripts/import-icmal-budget.cjs` parses İcmal's 2026 column (line items = non-empty Group ≠ "Group"; subtotals skipped), maps it into "Azərşəkər 2026 Budget" (kind=budget): **revenue/COGS by product → child company** (sugar beet→AZSF [AZSF sells only beet], other crops→EDEN, glucose/fructose/starch/lab/by-product→CPC, beer raw→MALT); **overhead (Logistics/S&M/OPEX) + investment subsidy split pro-rata by product-revenue** across the revenue children (the holding-rollup view only matches LEAF children, so holding-level lines never display); **subsidies included** as other income (farming+product→EDEN). Annual ÷ 12 → monthly; costs stored as positive magnitude to match the actuals convention. **Written: 2700 BudgetLines, 75 CoA accounts**; checksums exact vs İcmal subtotals (sales 58,880,102 + subsidies 13,153,098 = revenue 72,033,200; COGS 38,699,923; opex 19,017,618; net ≈ İcmal EBITDA 13.9M). Dry-run-default + user-approved preview before write (real financial data). **Execution % now non-zero** (full-year plan ÷ actuals-to-date): Доходы 13% (72.0M plan / 9.5M actual), COGS 20%, Расходы 24%. **Open follow-up:** the 2026 actuals are only ~9.5M revenue (low for mid-year) — likely partially loaded; worth confirming actuals completeness. Below-EBITDA (depreciation/interest/tax) intentionally excluded from the operating budget.

- **2026-06-02 (Decouple — Y5b-full: AI import understands actual vs budget + routes each sheet)** — The AI import now *knows* whether a sheet is realized ACTUALs or a forward BUDGET and writes it to the matching plan. **Chain:** Y5a `sectionContext` (per-sheet) → `sheet-classifier.withPlanKind()` stamps `SheetClassification.planKind` ("actual"|"budget") via the new exported `planKindForSheet(dataType, section)` decision table (section wins; else PLF/BS/CF→actual, SALES/SALES_FORECAST/**BUDGET_ACTUALS**→budget) → multi-file orchestrator passes `targetPlanKind: cls.planKind` into the handler (`AdapterRunInput.targetPlanKind`) → `production-adapter-registry` resolves a **per-(org,year,kind)** context cache and `resolveOrgContext(…, kind)` returns the right plan. **Critical bug fixed:** the 11 handlers did `ctx = ctxRef.value ?? ensureCtx()` — a single shared `ctxRef` that, once populated by the first sheet, routed EVERY later sheet to that first kind (budget rows → actuals plan, silently). All 11 now call `ensureCtx()` (per-kind cache). `BUDGET_ACTUALS` routes to budget so execution % (Σactual ÷ Σplanned within one plan) computes. **Single-file `/api/import/ai-auto` is classify-only preview (no writes)** → unaffected. tsc 0; **+15 tests** (4 `planKindForSheet` decision-table + 2 registry per-kind no-cross-contamination/back-compat; 2899 risk+import+budgeting green). **Verified live (read-only):** actuals plan "Azərşəkər 2026 Actuals" = 5913 BudgetLines + 959 BS (terminal source, populated); budget plan empty (0% execution is now *honest* — no 2026 budget entered yet, not a bug). This **completes the decouple** (Y1–Y5): terminal P&L ← actuals plan; budgeting execution ← budget plan; AI import routes each sheet to the correct plan. Remaining = the user loading real 2026 budget data into "Azərşəkər 2026 Budget".
- **2026-06-02 (Decouple — Y5b-core: import resolves the plan by KIND, not name)** — Caught + fixed a latent regression Y3 introduced: `resolveOrgContext` (the import's plan resolver) found its target by `DEFAULT_PLAN_NAME = "Azərşəkər 2026 Budget"`, which after the Y3 relabel is the NEW **empty budget** plan — so a financial re-import would have written ACTUALS into the budget plan (which the terminal's kind="actual" filter then ignores). Fixed: `resolveOrgContext(prisma, org, year, kind="actual")` now resolves by `(year, kind)` (create-name "Azərşəkər {year} {Actuals|Budget}"); the registry caller defaults to "actual". **Verified live:** `resolveOrgContext(2026)` → "Azərşəkər 2026 Actuals" (kind=actual, 732 lines), NOT the empty budget. tsc 0; 33 import-pipeline tests green. **Remaining (Y5b-full):** route SALES/budget sheets → the kind="budget" plan (thread Y5a's `sectionContext` through the classification → handler ctx; resolve the SALES handler's plan target). Financial-actuals routing is now correct + safe.
- **2026-06-02 (Decouple — Y5a: import section-context detection)** — `sheet-meta-extractor`: `SheetMeta` now carries `sectionContext` ("actual"|"budget"|"kpi"|"capex"|null), derived by `extractWorkbookMeta` walking sheets in order + tracking the nearest preceding separator ("Actual >>>"→actual, "KPI >>>"→kpi, "CAPEX >>>"→capex) via the new `separatorSection()` helper. This is the deterministic signal to route a PLF/BS/CF sheet under "Actual >>>" → the actuals plan vs a budget/sales-plan sheet → the budget plan. Additive field, **no behavior change yet**; +1 unit test (Guvven Fin shape: PLF CPC→actual, Farming KPI→kpi, CAPEX_Farm→capex); the sheet-classifier test's SheetMeta builder updated. tsc 0; 24 sheet-meta+classifier tests green. **NEXT (Y5b):** consume `sectionContext` in the multi-file orchestrator → route actual financial sheets to the kind="actual" plan, budget/sales-plan sheets to the kind="budget" plan (thread both plan IDs through the handler ctx). This is the substantive routing wire-up.

- **2026-06-02 (Client feedback #2 — IFRS check v3: statement linkage P&L↔equity SHIPPED)** — User: «делай сцепку». Built the cross-statement linkage previously deferred — but FIRST verified the identity holds on real data (decision-stress-test before coding): the equity "current-year profit/loss" line should equal the P&L net result (clean-surplus identity). It ties to the **cent** for MALT & CPC (gap 0) but diverges for AZSF (gap 536,149) & EDEN (217,811). Same formula ties exactly for 2 → the formula is correct and AZSF/EDEN have a **real reconciliation gap** (likely plan-vs-actual / period coverage). So designed as **pass / warn-for-review / skip — NEVER a hard fail** (a divergence may be legitimate timing, so flagging ≠ declaring the data wrong — protects against false-reds, the user's stated distrust) + convention-agnostic (ties under signed OR natural convention; 1%/min-1000 tol). New 8th check `pnl_equity_linkage`: identifies the current-year equity line by name (EN/RU/AZ regex), compares to `rev−cogs−opex`. **This finally differentiates the companies — directly answering «почему все 100%»:** MALT/CPC = 100 (linkage pass), AZSF/EDEN = **94** (linkage ▲ review, gap surfaced). - **2026-06-02 (Client feedback #2 — IFRS check v2: 2 deeper structural checks + discrimination proof)** — User challenged the all-100% result: «он реально правильно проверяет? почему все 100%». **Proved it discriminates** on REAL CPC data (in-memory, no DB writes) — broke the data 5 ways → score 100 fell to 70/80/90 (drop equity → balances ✖; assets +10% → balances ✖ residual 2 577 216; COGS into OpEx → ▲; strip D&A names → ▲; drop revenue → ✖). Explained the 100s are honest: MALT/AZSF/EDEN/CPC were imported from clean complete workbooks, so structurally there's nothing to fail. **Then raised the bar** with 2 new IAS 1 checks (5 → 7): **(6) current vs non-current split** — assets+liabilities carry a `subType` (IAS 1 §60; warn if a section lacks it) + **(7) equity broken into components** — ≥2 distinct equity accounts (IAS 1 §54; warn on a single lumped equity line). Perturbation-verified on real data: strip asset subType → current/non-current ▲ (93), lump equity → equity-composition ▲ (93), drop equity → balances ✖ + sections ▲ (75); all 4 real companies stay 100 (genuinely well-structured). **Deferred to v3 (transparency):** hard value-linkage (P&L net income ↔ equity movement) — `BudgetLine.plannedAmount` isn't period-aligned to BS snapshots and `rev−cogs−opex` ≠ statutory net income (tax/interest live inside "expense"); 3/4 companies run a *real* net loss, so a wrong linkage would paint clean data red — exactly the distrust the user flagged. New BS-snapshot fields (subType coverage + distinct equity-account count) threaded through `buildIfrsInput` + route (`subType`/`accountId` selected). 35 lib+handler tests; +2 i18n check blocks + 2 field labels ×3 (parity green); tsc 0. Verified live (CPC → 7 rows, equity components 4).

New pure lib `src/lib/audit/ifrs-checks.ts`: `runIfrsChecks(input)` → 5 IAS 1 structural checks — **(1)** balance sheet balances (Assets = Liabilities + Equity, convention-agnostic: accepts both natural all-positive and the codebase's signed trial-balance convention, 0.5%/min-1 tolerance), **(2)** all three BS sections present, **(3)** revenue recognised, **(4)** cost of sales separated from operating expenses (IAS 1 by-function), **(5)** D&A identifiable as its own line (multilingual name fallback EN/RU/AZ when `category` absent) — plus a 0-100 conformance score (warn = ½). `buildIfrsInput` shapes raw DB rows (distinct-account counting; D&A detection by `category="depreciation"` OR name regex). Restyled the shell + header to the terminal aesthetic (navy `#0A0E27` + `text-gray-200` base, gradient header, Bell in an amber chip, ghost close), aligned the accent `#FFB020`→`#FFB800`, `text-muted-foreground`→gray, input borders → `white/15`. tsc 0; 25 AISubscriptions tests pass. Verified live. On-demand overlay → outside the visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (HotkeyToolbar — collapse the tail into the ⌘K dropdown)** — User: «верхние hotkey не раскрывай до конца … после import остальное сверни чтоб был выпадающий». The 2026-05-31 tweak had surfaced ALL ~24 hotkeys inline (`overflow-x-auto` horizontal scroll). Reverted to a collapse: items are shown inline through **"import"** (positional cut, user's choice via AskUserQuestion), and everything after folds into the existing ⌘K palette/dropdown — EXCEPT `recompute` + `impact-scan`, which stay pinned because they're stateful action buttons (live progress / running label) a click-to-close menu can't show feedback for. `visibleHotkeys`/`overflowHotkeys` now positional; the palette renders `overflowHotkeys` (not a `priority` filter) so pinned items after import don't vanish, and its group list gained `sector`. tsc 0; HotkeyToolbar tests updated (the obsolete "surface ALL" test → "collapses post-import into palette"; recompute tests unchanged since recompute stays inline) → 12 pass. **Known caveat (flagged to user):** "through import" is still ~13 items + actions, which exceeds ~1280px, so the bar still scrolls slightly at narrow widths; collapsing the analysis/social cluster too would make it truly fit — offered as a follow-up toggle.

- **2026-06-01 (Scenario create — guided builder so a non-programmer can add a scenario)** — User: «json почему не убираешь коды? как тут добавить сценарий не программисту?». The Create form defaulted to the legacy `adjustments[]` JSON template, so the friendly editor (which only showed for `shock` scenarios) didn't appear and a non-technical client faced raw JSON. Fixed: (1) `OVERRIDES_TEMPLATE` now starts as a `shock` (output price −10%) so the friendly editor shows immediately on Create; (2) the editor became a **builder** — `shockView` renders for any `shock` (even empty), each parameter has a **× remove**, a **"+ Add parameter"** picker adds an unused lever / feed-anchored target / costRigidity / imported-input share, and the feed target gains a **metric picker** (Brent / AZN / FAO). Raw JSON stays under "Advanced". A non-programmer builds a scenario entirely with dropdowns + number inputs. +6 i18n keys ×3 locales; ScenarioFormModal tests 11→15 (create shows builder / add target / remove lever→empty disables Save / metric picker). **Verified live** (Playwright): New-scenario form shows the builder with a starter lever; added a Brent target via the picker — no JSON. tsc 0. Overlay → outside the visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Data Sources Catalog — card prose localized; English UI no longer shows Russian)** — User (on `/budgeting/admin/data-sources`): «тут тоже нет перевода». The page chrome already used `next-intl` (correctly English in the EN-locale UI), but the 12 source cards rendered hardcoded Russian-only fields (`whatItIsRu`/`businessValueRu`/`sampleLatest.interpretation`/`cadenceRu`) — so an English UI showed Russian card bodies. Fix: new `src/lib/intel/sources-catalog-i18n.ts` carries EN prose for all 12 sources keyed by `sourceCode` + a `localizedSource(entry, locale)` picker (RU → catalog's native fields, EN → this map, AZ → EN fallback so an AZ user never sees Russian); the catalog itself is untouched (adapters still read `*Ru`). `DataSourcesCatalogView` now renders `localizedSource(...)` for name/whatItIs/businessValue/interpretation/cadence. **Verified live** (Playwright, EN locale): cards now English ("Official daily AZN exchange rates…"), no Russian leak. +5 unit tests incl. an "every source has EN" guard. tsc 0. **Follow-up:** native AZ card prose (currently AZ→EN fallback) — flag for AZ_TRANSLATIONS_REVIEW.

- **2026-06-01 (Crisis sim — slow-hint + hard timeout so it never looks frozen)** — User: «не генерирует» (Run crisis stuck on "Modelling crisis…", no brief). Diagnosed via the dev log + a 3× timing probe: the `simulate?mode=drivers` call spiked to **55s** on one run, then measured **406/348/1350 ms** — a transient CPU-contention spike (many concurrent sessions on the machine), NOT a code bug or a value-190 blowup (the sim does fixed work regardless of shock magnitude). The real flaw was UX: `runDrivers` had no progress feedback or timeout, so a slow run read as frozen. Added: a "still computing… (high load)" hint after 8s (`crisis-slow-hint`) and a 90s `AbortController` hard timeout that surfaces a clear retry message (`crisisTimeout`) instead of hanging forever. Happy path re-verified live (brief still generates). +2 i18n keys ×3 locales. tsc 0; 49 ScenarioPanel tests. On-demand overlay → outside visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Crisis brief — AI language switch re-narrates immediately)** — User: «я меняю язык но ничего не происходит». The RU/EN/AZ pills only set `aiLang` for the NEXT run; an already-generated brief kept its original-language narrative. Fix: cache the narrative inputs from the last run in `narrativeInputRef`, extract a shared `postNarrative(language, brief)` helper (used by both `runDrivers` and the pills), and on a language-pill click — when a brief is already shown — re-POST `/narrative` in the new language right away (numbers/cascade/score-swing stay; only the narrative text + mitigations re-fetch, with the loading indicator). **Verified live** (Playwright): Run crisis (RU) → click EN → a fresh narrative POST fires + the "generating" state shows. tsc 0; 54 scenario tests. On-demand overlay → outside visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Scenario detail — "Active" parameters chip shows the real saved value)** — User (after editing Brent target): «я поднял до 190, тут где-то нужно чтоб показывало что 190 вычитывает». Diagnosed: the save works (verified live — input 100→190 → DB 190), but the only number reflecting the edit was the post-Run-crisis feed anchor; the big title + description are static text ("$140") and never update, so the active target wasn't visible before running. Added an **ACTIVE:** chip row in the detail header, derived from the scenario's saved `overrides.shock` via new exported helper `readActiveShock` — shows the target (e.g. "Brent crude (target) → 190 $/bbl", value highlighted amber) and/or direct levers as signed % (price −40%) + costRigidity. `handleFormSaved` already refetches, so after edit+Save the chip updates immediately. **Verified live** (Playwright): chip read 140, edited→190→Save, chip re-read 190; demo data restored to 140 after. +5 unit tests (`scenario-active-shock.test.ts`) + 1 i18n key ×3 locales. tsc 0; 54 scenario tests. On-demand overlay → outside visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Scenario save — server now accepts `shock` overrides; "Validation error" fixed)** — User (live, editing BRENT_TO_140 → 197 / fxShock): the dark form + friendly editor worked, but Save showed red **"Validation error"**. Root cause: the API routes' Zod schema (`POST /api/scenarios` + `PATCH /api/scenarios/[id]`) only accepted `overrides: { adjustments[] }` and **rejected `{ shock }`** — so every crisis/shock scenario 400'd on save even though the client validator + the simulate engine both accept `shock`. (The catalog scenarios were seeded directly, bypassing the route, which is why this only surfaced on edit.) Fix: new shared `src/lib/risk/scenario-overrides-schema.ts` — `ScenarioOverridesSchema` accepts the legacy `adjustments[]` form OR a `shock{}` form (target with `metric`/`value`/`drives∈{fx,price,inputCost}` and/or direct levers `revenue/price/inputCost/fx/yieldShock` + `costRigidity`/`assumedImportShare`), mirroring `ScenarioShock`/`isValidTarget`; both routes wired to it (removed the duplicated local `AdjustmentSchema`). Tests: 9 schema unit + 3 PATCH handler (200 BRENT-target / 200 priceShock-lever / 400 empty-shock). tsc 0; scenarios routes + schema 46/46.

- **2026-06-01 (ScenarioFormModal — dark redesign + friendly editor for direct levers)** — User (live demo, showing PRICE_DROP_40 edit): «тут не изменил и опять json коды показывают». Two gaps: (1) the edit FORM was still on light shadcn tokens (the redesign had only covered the ScenarioPanel selector, not the create/edit form) — converted it to the same dark terminal panel (shell `#0A0E27` + `text-gray-200` base, dark inputs `bg-white/[0.03]`, gradient header, ghost close, sticky dark footer, full light-token sweep). (2) the friendly editor only handled feed-anchored `shock.target` scenarios; lever-style scenarios like `PRICE_DROP_40` (`{shock:{priceShock:-0.4}}`) and `DROUGHT_2026` (`{shock:{revenueShock,yieldShock,costRigidity}}`) still showed raw JSON. Extended it: `shockView` now also surfaces the direct P&L levers (`revenueShock`/`priceShock`/`inputCostShock`/`fxShock`/`yieldShock`) as labeled **percentage** inputs (−0.4 ⇄ "−40 %"), plus `costRigidity`/`assumedImportShare` as 0–1 inputs — raw JSON stays under "Advanced". `patchShock` generalized to a mutate-callback. +6 i18n keys ×3 locales. Tests: ScenarioFormModal 8→11 (added price-shock lever shows-not-JSON / lever %→fraction write / multi-lever+rigidity surfaced). **Verified live via Playwright** — captured the PRICE_DROP_40 edit form: dark panel, "SCENARIO PARAMETERS → Output price change: −40 %", JSON collapsed. tsc 0; 60 tests across form+panel. On-demand overlay → outside visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (ScenarioPanel — dark professional redesign)** — User (live demo): «ужасные цвета … переработай UX/UI, используй дизайнерские скилы». The Scenario Analysis (What-if) modal was built on light shadcn tokens (`bg-background`→white, default text→black) so it rendered as a glaring bright box on the dark Bloomberg terminal. Reworked into a cohesive dark terminal panel: shell `#0A0E27` + `text-gray-200` base (fixes all the invisible black-on-dark text in one move) + `border-white/10` + ring/shadow; gradient header with the lab icon in an amber chip; list/detail split by a hairline divider. Scenario cards: dark `bg-white/[0.02]` with real hover (border + bg lift) and a selected state that gets an **amber inset accent-bar** + amber text. Detail: white title, mono code·period chip, description in a card with an amber left-accent rule. CTAs re-tiered — **Run crisis** is now a confident **filled-red hero** (`#FF4757`, white text, soft glow) instead of washed pink; **Quick calc** a ghost button with an amber bolt; **AI RU/EN/AZ** a segmented control; **Apply** aligned to the terminal teal `#00D4AA`. Swept every remaining light token (`text-muted-foreground`→gray-400, `border-border`/`border-input`→white/10, `bg-muted*`→white/5) so the whole modal + its brief/delta sub-panels are consistent. **Verified live** (Playwright, logged in, real DROUGHT_2026 run): captured the selector + the full detail/brief view. tsc 0; ScenarioPanel + ScenarioFormModal suites 57/57. Modal is an on-demand overlay → outside the HeatMap visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Scenario edit — friendly shock editor + validator bug fix)** — User (live demo): «что за коды … я не могу менять тут цены, хочу не 140 а 120» — editing a crisis scenario (BRENT_TO_140) showed raw JSON and Save greyed out on any change. **Root cause:** `ScenarioFormModal.validateJson` only accepted the legacy `adjustments[]` override format and **rejected** the Phase-2 `shock{...}` format that every catalog crisis scenario uses (BRENT_TO_140 / AZN_DEVAL_* / DROUGHT_2026 / SUGAR_*) — so the moment the user changed the target number, `jsonError` set and the Save button disabled. The simulate route already reads `shock` from the saved overrides, so the edit *would* have taken effect — it just couldn't be saved. **Fix:** validator now accepts `adjustments[]` OR a valid `shock` (`hasShock` from `scenario-shock.ts`). **Plus** (closes the recurring «сделать редактирование интерактивным, не кодинг» feedback) a friendly editor: when overrides carry a `shock.target`, the modal renders labeled inputs — a number field for the target value with the metric's unit (e.g. «Brent crude (target): 120 $/bbl»), a plain-language **Effect** dropdown (raises input cost / FX devaluation / output price), and an imported-input-share field when present — with the raw JSON tucked under a collapsible «Advanced». Inputs two-way-sync with the JSON (JSON stays source of truth). +12 i18n keys ×3 locales; new `ScenarioFormModal.test.tsx` (8 cases: friendly editor shows for shock / hidden for legacy / value edit rewrites JSON + keeps Save enabled / raw shock JSON accepted / malformed still rejected / effect select / importShare input / PATCH sends edited value). tsc 0. Modal is an on-demand overlay → outside the visual baseline.

- **2026-06-01 (Client-feedback #5 — intuitive indicator search in HeatMap Panel 2)** — Client (Azik demo): «индикаторов много… надо во втором панели сделать интуитивный поиск чтоб он понимал что я хочу даже если слово не точное». Added a second toolbar input `find indicator…` (beside the existing `/` company-row filter) that fuzzy-filters the matrix COLUMNS. New pure helper `src/features/terminal/lib/indicator-search.ts`: token-based, **multilingual** (matches `code` + de-underscored code + `nameEn`/`nameRu`/`nameAz`, so a RU/AZ/EN word finds the indicator regardless of UI locale), **inexact-tolerant** tiers exact → prefix → reverse-prefix (declension: stored «гектар» matches typed «гектар**у**») → substring → char-drift (typos «margn»→margin), **AND across typed words**, original column order preserved. Wired through `use-heat-map-model.ts` (local `indicatorQuery` state; an active query takes precedence and bypasses «hide unknown» so a searched-for column always surfaces). Empty-match shows a `No indicators match` row; live count `N/total`; Esc/× clears. +6 i18n keys × 3 locales + vitest mock labels. Tests: 13 matcher unit + 5 happy-dom UI integration (assert real column reduction on «margin» / «гектар» / latin «per ha» / clear-restores-all). tsc 0; targeted suite 96/96. **Visual baseline regenerated** (new toolbar input shifts the Panel-2 toggle cluster right; regen also absorbs cell-value data-drift from the 2026-06-01 ProMalt recompute since the last baseline — cells are unmasked by design). `BASELINE UPDATE` token in commit. Residual: the HeatMap visual baseline retains a rare cold-load flake (1/9 runs) from unmasked live cells mid-recompute — pre-existing, not introduced here.

- **2026-05-31 (Terminal panels — full i18n sweep: the 4 remaining hardcoded-English panels)** — User: «переведи все панели без перевода». Scanned all terminal components for `useTranslations=0` + user-facing English; found 4 fully-untranslated panels and internationalized them (en/ru/az): **FxExposurePanel** (CFO hedging — net exposure, per-currency P&L, hedge hints), **AgroDashboardPanel** (agro KPI tiles + empty-state with rich-text `KPI GO`/path links via `t.rich`), **AgronomyEntryPanel** (KPI entry form — metric picker, labels, save/validation), **ConcentrationPanel** (customer/supplier HHI + top-counterparty tables). +90 keys × 3 locales added to `messages/` (fxExposure 17, agroDashboard 23, agronomyEntry 23, concentration 27). The 3 agro/concentration panels were refactored by parallel subagents (returning translation tables; merged centrally with a shared glossary for cross-panel term consistency — Revenue→Выручка/Gəlir, COGS→Себестоимость/MMD, etc.); FX done inline. Verified live: both agro pop-outs render fully in RU with rich-text intact (no literal tags, no raw keys); all t() keys cross-checked present in messages. tsc 0; full suite 5474; visual gate green (panels are pop-outs/on-demand — outside the baseline viewport). Excluded (not panels): `RiskMatrixPdf.tsx` (PDF export template) + the pop-out wrapper chrome title (`BUDGETPRO TERMINAL · <kind>` in terminal-panel/[id]/layout) — noted as minor follow-ups.

- **2026-05-31 (What-If preview modal — full i18n: scenario chips, tabs, driver labels)** — User feedback: the What-If preview was only partly translated — the header/buttons were localized but the module-level constants (`SCENARIO_GROUPS`, `PRESETS`) carried hardcoded English. Translated: the 4 driver-group tabs (FX Rates/Commodities/Macro/Agricultural → Курсы валют/Сырьё/Макро/Агро), the 6 preset scenario chips (AZN Peg Break/OPEC+ Breakdown/Black Sea Grain Crisis/Turkish Lira Crash/Kura-Araz Drought/Full External Shock + their hover descriptions), and the 15 driver labels (AZN per 1 USD, Brent Crude, …). Rendered via `t(\`whatif.groups.${group.key}\`)` / `whatif.vars.${v.key}` / `whatif.presets.${preset.key}` (+ `presetDescs`) — the constants kept their `key`/EN reference, text now comes from i18n. +33 keys × 3 locales (en/ru/az) added to `messages/`. Verified live: modal renders fully in RU (chips + tabs + driver labels all Russian). tsc 0; full suite 5474.

- **2026-05-31 (Terminal readability — signals legibility + all hotkeys surfaced + command-bar contrast)** — User feedback: the Risk Terminal top strip was hard to read + hotkeys were buried. **Signals strip (`SignalsStrip.tsx`):** chips now severity-colored (red=high / amber=medium border+tint) for at-a-glance scanning; label text bumped `foreground/90`→`gray-100 font-medium` 11→12px wider (was truncating dim), the `→ SCENARIO` code `muted-foreground`→`sky-300/90 font-mono` (was the dimmest, near-invisible), bigger severity dot. **Hotkeys (`HotkeyToolbar.tsx`):** surfaced EVERY hotkey directly in the (horizontally-scrolling) toolbar instead of hiding 15+ behind the ⌘K palette — `visibleHotkeys = hotkeys`; the palette auto-hides when nothing's overflow; toolbar text `gray-500`→`gray-300` 10→11px, button borders `gray-800`→`gray-700`, label brighter. **Command bar (`CommandBar.tsx`):** `[cmd]`/`CO:` `gray-500`→`gray-400`, placeholder `gray-600`→`gray-500`. **Visual gate:** also masked the live audit-events ticker (`AuditTicker.tsx` `data-volatile="true"`) — its timestamp/event content was a latent data-drift flake in the baseline (caught when the regen + verify run diverged on the bottom EVENTS line). Baseline regenerated + 2 determinism passes green. +3 HotkeyToolbar tests updated (overflow commands now assert direct-in-toolbar, not palette). tsc 0; full suite 5474. **BASELINE UPDATE: layout** (intentional top-strip redesign + events-ticker mask — not data drift).

- **2026-05-31 (Sparkline aggregation parity — closed the architect's residual nit)** — Threaded `aggregation` through the full sparkline pipeline so the 12-month trailing viz resolves operationalFact inputs with the same snapshot/flow flag as the spot value (`IndicatorForSparkline` + `evaluateAt`'s per-period buildContext callback + `bridgeRecomputeBuildContext` + the `recompute.ts` inline definition + the offline `compute-sparklines.ts` worker's select+def). Previously the sparkline always meaned — harmless today (snapshot metrics are single-fact-per-month) but a soft invariant since `OperationalFact` has no `@@unique(companyId,metric,date)`; threading is the schema-safe close (the `@@unique` alternative would have needed a dup-check + import-upsert review). +2 regression tests (`sparkline.test.ts`: capture-stub asserts the flag is forwarded — snapshot when set, flow default). tsc 0; full suite **5474**.

- **2026-05-31 (operationalFact aggregation — regex → per-IndicatorDefinition `aggregation` flag; schema migration, architect-reviewed)** — Replaced the central `SNAPSHOT_METRIC_RE` regex (the snapshot-vs-flow classifier behind the CPC court fix) with a seed-declared, data-driven `IndicatorDefinition.aggregation` column ("snapshot"|"flow", default flow) — the architect's recommended long-term fix, done now per the user's go-ahead. **Migration** `20260531165506_add_indicator_aggregation` (additive ADD COLUMN, default 'flow' — non-locking, backfills existing rows safely). The resolver now branches on `ctx.aggregation`; threaded from `IndicatorDefinition.aggregation` through `ResolverCtx` + `buildContext` + **all 5 recompute defLike builders** (recompute-trigger [main prod], `api/indicators` sync+async, matrix/preview, scenario-rederive) and their `select`s, plus `scripts/backfill-quarter-month-ivs.ts` (the one builder the architect caught omitting it). **12 indicators flagged `snapshot`** in the seeds (4 compliance: LEGAL_CASES_ACTIVE/TOTAL, AUDIT_CLOSED_PCT/MAJOR_OPEN; 7 agro: DROUGHT_RISK, YIELD_PER_HA, SUGAR_CONTENT, WATER/FERTILIZER_INTENSITY, BUYER_CONCENTRATION, HARVEST_PROGRESS; 1 food-proc: FP_EXTRACTION_RATE) + backfilled to existing global rows via `scripts/set-snapshot-aggregation.ts` (idempotent; 12 matched). Verified NO indicator mixes snapshot+flow operationalFact inputs, so the indicator-level flag is sufficient; the regex is deleted outright (no fallback) — a new "the FLAG, not the metric name, drives aggregation" test locks the rot-proofing. **End-to-end verified live:** CPC LEGAL_CASES_ACTIVE recompute via the flag path = 8 (latest), not 7 (mean). **Architect APPROVE-WITH-NITS:** the one real finding (backfill script select omitting the field → silent re-average vector) fixed; sparkline path left on flow-mean (harmless — per-month single-fact; soft invariant logged in CARRYOVER). tsc 0; full suite **5472**.

- **2026-05-31 (Soft-delete read audit — PHASE 2 done: write/export/enumeration paths; the whole class is now closed)** — Finished the soft-delete sweep started in phase 1 (passive financial displays). Phase 2 covers the remaining reads of soft-delete tables (`BudgetLine`/`BalanceSheetLine`/`CashFlowEntry`) that lacked `deletedAt:null` — none are passive CFO displays, but several would propagate or mis-report archived rows: **`create-version`** (the source-plan `lines` include was unfiltered → cloning a plan version copied archived budget lines into the new version and resurrected them — highest-risk; +regression test locking `include.lines.where.deletedAt:null`), **`apply-templates`** (archived account treated as "already present" → skipped on re-apply), **`cash-flow/generate`** (budgetLine projection injected phantom flows + the balance/alert read distorted by archived entries), **`csv-template`** (exported archived lines), **`period-snapshot`** (enumerated plans/companies with only archived lines), **`completeness-checker`** (onboarding "is data loaded?" counts on budgetLine ×3 / budgetLine.findMany / balanceSheetLine / cashFlowEntry — `salesBudgetLine`/`cOGSBudgetLine` skipped, no soft-delete column). All uniform `deletedAt:null`. tsc 0; full suite **5473**. With phase 1 (`balance-sheet`/`cash-flow`/`cash-flow/odds`/`availability` GETs + `report-engine` + `company-financials-snapshot`) this closes the entire soft-delete read class; terminal recompute + `pnl`/`analytics` were already correct.

- **2026-05-31 (EDEN land registry — ×11 inflation fixed: 431 parcels/225,236 ha → 17/22,596; source misclassification + recurrence guard)** — Closed the confirmed land bug. The terminal strategic-context panel summed `Company.settings.landParcels` and showed EDEN at **225,236 ha / 431 parcels** (2,252 km² — physically absurd). Root cause was NOT parse-math: the AI multi-import had classified the Farming-strategy **"Torpaq"** sheet (which repeats the land registry once per forecast year, 2027-2037) as a land-registry and ran `parseLandRegistrySheet` on it — so `sequenceNumber` held YEARS and the registry was stored ×11 (`landRegistrySource: multi-import:Torpaq`). The real registry is **`Çıxarışların uçotu.xlsx` → 17 parcels / 22,595.7 ha** (= the user-confirmed ~22,596 exactly; proper seq 1-17; 5 regions). **Fix:** (1) re-imported the correct source via `apply-azseker-land.ts` — EDEN now **17 parcels / 22,596 ha** with regions populated (Ağcabədi/Beyləqan/Yevlax/Füzuli/Şəmkir), verified live; (2) added a **guard** in `parseLandRegistryFromAoa`: if >50% of parsed parcels carry year-like sequence numbers (1990-2100) it returns empty + a warning instead of persisting ~N× garbage — so re-running the multi-import on "Torpaq" can no longer overwrite the good data (verified: Torpaq → 0 parcels rejected, Çıxarışların → 17 accepted). +2 unit tests; tsc 0; full suite **5473**. The user confirmed EDEN's real leased land ≈ 22,596 ha (`hectaresPlanted`=4,000 is the separate planted-sugarcane figure). This was the last open data-correctness finding from the soft-data reconciliation pass. Forward-forecast was then verified too (`parseIcmalSheet` İcmal = 10 years 2026-2035 + terminal; DB `Org.settings.forwardForecast` breakdown matches source exactly) — so the soft-data pass is COMPLETE: audit ✓, court ✓, risk ✓, land ✓, forward ✓.

- **2026-05-31 (operationalFactResolver — LATEST-for-snapshot fix; CPC active court cases corrected 7→8; architect-reviewed)** — Fixed the root cause behind the CPC LEGAL_CASES_ACTIVE finding (user chose the resolver fix over a data-delete). The resolver always averaged in-period OperationalFacts; for **snapshot/stock** metrics this blends successive point-in-time snapshots from re-imports (CPC: 6 @ 2026-05-01 stale + 8 @ 2026-12-31 → mean 7 instead of 8). Now `SNAPSHOT_METRIC_RE` (recompute-resolvers-a.ts) routes snapshot metrics to **latest-by-date** while additive/flow metrics keep averaging. Scope: compliance/legal/audit counts (`LEGAL_CASES_`, `AUDIT_CLOSED_PCT`, `AUDIT_MAJOR_OPEN`, `court_disputes_`, `audit_findings_`) **and** intensive ratios/indices (suffixes `_pct` / `_per_ha` / `_index`: yield_per_ha, sugar_content_pct, drought_index, …) — the latter added per architect review (semantically wrong to mean-average an intensive quantity; latent today only because each is written single-fact). **Applied live:** recomputed CPC → IND **8 amber**; `verify-compliance-vs-source.ts` COURT now ALL MATCH. **Architect: APPROVE-WITH-NITS** — both nits closed (extended allow-list to the intensive metrics; corrected the stale `recompute.ts:22` "Avg of OperationalFact.value" doc). Blast radius verified clean (no consumer reads `aggregates.operational_fact.avg` as a literal mean). +6 unit tests (snapshot→latest incl. unordered, intensive-ratio→latest, flow→mean, single-fact, regex both directions); tsc 0; full suite **5470**. Long-term (CARRYOVER): move stock/flow to a per-`IndicatorDefinition.aggregation` flag so the central regex can't rot.

Root cause nailed: the parcels' `sequenceNumber` ∈ {2027…2037} = **11 consecutive YEARS**, each holding the SAME ~39-parcel registry (per-year Σha ≈ 20,461–20,516) → the land registry was duplicated once per forecast year. **Correct total = one year ≈ 20,500 ha**, matching the user-confirmed ~22,596 ha (NOT 225,236; `hectaresPlanted`=4,000 is the separate planted figure, so the earlier "22,596=planted" guess was wrong). Fix next turn: trace + de-duplicate the importer that wrote year-keyed parcels (likely the multi-file AI-import soft handler — `apply-azseker-land`/`parseLandRegistrySheet` THROWS on the current source + 26≠431), dedupe stored parcels to one year, re-verify Σha ≈ 20,500 + populate region (empty; district in `lessor`).
